DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player constantly moves forward through a corridor and can tap to spawn an echo that retraces the exact path the player just traveled, clearing any obstacles it contacts while the player keeps moving. The satisfaction comes from planning a route, then watching the echo rip through hazards you just avoided.

## Player
A glowing cursor or ship that always progresses forward on a dark neon‑lit track. The player steers laterally (or up/down) to dodge obstacles and taps/clicks to create an echo. **Verbs:** steer, tap (spawn echo).

## Core loop
1. The player is automatically propelled forward.
2. The player steers to weave between incoming obstacles.
3. After a short cooldown, the player can tap to spawn an echo at the current position.
4. The echo follows the exact recent path of the player for a few seconds, destroying any obstacles it meets.
5. The player continues forward, using the cleared corridor to advance further.
The loop repeats every 1–3 seconds, with the player deciding when and where to place the echo to maximize obstacle removal.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Static blocks:** Fixed rectangular shapes that sit in the track; colliding ends the run.
- **Sweeping barriers:** Horizontal or vertical bars that move back‑and‑forth, telegraphing their path with a faint line before moving.
- **Spiked spikes:** Pointy protrusions that appear briefly then retract; they kill on contact.
- **Delayed mines:** Appear a moment after the player passes, forcing the player to use the echo to clear the space before they become active.

## Score
- Destroying an obstacle with the echo awards points.
- Successfully navigating past a hazard without taking damage awards a small bonus.
- Each full echo that clears at least three obstacles grants a combo multiplier.

## Win and lose
- **Lose:** The player collides with any obstacle while the game is in "playing" state.
- **Win:** Survive until a predetermined distance (e.g., 2000 units) or time limit (e.g., 2 minutes), at which point the game displays a victory screen.

## Feel
A steady but tension‑building pace that ramps up as obstacles become more frequent. Neon‑glow visual style on a dark background, with crisp electronic sound effects for echo activation, obstacle destruction, and player death. The experience feels rhythmic and rewarding, like a dance of light and shadow.

## Why it's fun
The single, thrilling moment when your echo races along the path you just forged and vaporizes every obstacle in its wake.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold + meta.json — create header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md, stub <script> block, and generate matching meta.json file.
2. Game state objects — define player, hazards, score, and phase as plain object literals at the top of the script with initial values.
3. Animation loop — implement startLoop() using requestAnimationFrame with cancelAnimationFrame handling, wrap loop body in try/catch, call update(dt) and render(); clear canvas only.
4. Input handling — set up a keys Set populated by document keydown/keyup listeners, add R-key restart hook and restart button click hook, both invoking reset().
5. Player movement — implement dt‑based physics using key inputs, apply acceleration, cap at MAX_SPEED, apply friction, and clamp player position to canvas bounds.
6. Hazards — create hazard data structure, spawn rules with SAFE_RADIUS to avoid player at t=0, and motion scaled by dt.
7. Collision detection — detect player vs hazards collisions; on hit transition phase to "gameover" and stop active gameplay.
8. Score — increase score on discrete events, draw the score on the canvas at the top‑left.
9. Render — clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player (high‑contrast), and HUD each frame.
10. Game‑over overlay — dim background, display "Game Over", final score, and "Press R or click Restart" prompt.
11. Observable contract — populate window.__game each frame with { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective } where state is "playing" | "gameover" and objective is a concise description.
12. Audio — lazily initialise AudioContext in a beep() helper wrapped in try/catch, and hook beep calls into hit, score, and death events.

// === STATE ==============================================================
    // Player state, hazards array, score and phase.
    let player = { x: 400, y: 300, r: 10 };
    let hazards = []; // each hazard will be an object with its own properties
    let score = 0;
    let phase = "menu";
    // Configuration constants
    const SAFE_RADIUS = 150; // pixels
    const HAZARD_SPEED = 200; // pixels per second
    const HAZARD_SIZE = 12; // radius of hazard
    const HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL = 2; // seconds between spawns
    let hazardSpawnAccumulator = 0;
    // Canvas context
    const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
    const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");
    // === LOOP ==============================================================
    // Animation loop handling.
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();

    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
               lastTime = now;
               update(dt);
               render();
               publishState();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error("loop error", err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    function update(dt) {
      // Placeholder – game logic added later.
    }

    function render() {
      // Clear canvas.
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // Background.
      ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // Hazards.
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff5252";
      hazards.forEach(h => {
        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.arc(h.x, h.y, h.r || HAZARD_SIZE, 0, Math.PI * 2);
        ctx.fill();
      });
      // Player.
      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.r, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
      // HUD.
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif";
      ctx.textAlign = "left";
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
      // Game‑over overlay.
      if (phase === "gameover") {
        ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
        ctx.textAlign = "center";
        ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif";
        ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
        ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
        ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif";
        ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 16);
        ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
        ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif";
        ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 44);
      }
    }

    // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
    function beep(freq, durSec) {
      try {
        if (!window.audioCtx) window.audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
        const osc = window.audioCtx.createOscillator();
        const gain = window.audioCtx.createGain();
        osc.type = "square";
        osc.frequency.value = freq;
        gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, window.audioCtx.currentTime);
        gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, window.audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
        osc.connect(gain);
        gain.connect(window.audioCtx.destination);
        osc.start();
        osc.stop(window.audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      } catch (e) {
        // ignore audio errors
      }
    }

    // Stub observable contract – reflects current state.
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,
      score: score,
      player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible: true },
      objective: "Tap to spawn an echo that follows your path and destroys obstacles.",
    };

Now we need to produce the design text. Let's think about the game design.

Game: Echo Trail. Player moves forward automatically (like endless runner?), with path traced by the player. The player can "tap to spawn an echo" that follows exact same path the player just traveled, for few seconds, destroying obstacles it contacts while you continue moving forward.

Thus the concept: Player continuously moves forward through a corridor. They can tap (or click) to create a temporary echo that retraces the player's recent path, removing obstacles. The satisfying part is planning your path and then having the echo clean obstacles for you, giving a strategic feel.

Player: Represented as a glowing point or ship moving forward in a scrolling environment. Controls: Move left/right (or up/down) and tap to spawn echo. Maybe both mouse/touch.

Verbs: steer (move laterally), dash (maybe not), spawn echo.

Core loop: Each frame, player moves forward automatically; player steers to avoid obstacles; after a short cooldown, the player can tap to create an echo; the echo spawns at player's current location and follows the exact path that the player traveled during the last few seconds, moving along that path at same speed and destroying any obstacles it hits. Player must keep moving and planning when to spawn echo to clear upcoming obstacles. The loop cycles: navigate, create echo, watch echo clear obstacles, keep moving.

Hazards/Obstacles: static obstacles (blocks) placed on the path; moving obstacles (horizontal or vertical sweeps) that sweep across the lane; spiky obstacles that would kill on contact; maybe random "spike walls" or "crawlers". Could be an obstacle that appears after you pass, requiring you to use echo to clear if you overshoot.

Score: Each obstacle destroyed by the echo gives points; each obstacle avoided gives points; distance traveled; perhaps combos for multiple obstacles in one echo; also time survived.

Win/Lose: Player loses if collides with any obstacle while playing. Player wins after surviving a set distance or time (e.g., after 2 minutes) or after clearing a certain number of obstacles. Could be indefinite but we need win condition: maybe reaching a safe zone at end of level.

Feel: The vibe maybe neon retro futuristic, steady but tense, music with electronic pulses. Pace is steady but becomes frantic as hazards increase. Aesthetic: neon line art on dark background.

Why it's fun: The moment you watch your echo trace the path and wipe away hazards you set up, a satisfying "clean-up" feeling.

All good.

Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. // Stub observable contract – will be updated each frame.
```
window.__game = {
  state: phase,
  score: score,
  player: { x: player.x, y: player.y, visible: true },
  objective: "Tap to spawn an echo that follows your path and destroys obstacles.",
};

function publishState() {
  window.__game.state = phase;
  window.__game.score = score;
  window.__game.player.x = player.x;
  window.__game.player.y = player.y;
  const visible = player.x >= 0 && player.x <= canvas.width && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= canvas.height;
  window.__game.player.visible = visible;
}

// Input handling
const keys = new Set();
document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') { reset(); return; }
  keys.add(ev.key);
});
document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
  keys.delete(ev.key);
});
restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);

// Reset function
function reset() {
  // Cancel any existing loop
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  // Reset state
  player.x = canvas.width / 2;
  player.y = canvas.height / 2;
  hazards = [];
  score = 0;
  phase = "playing";
  // Reset timing
  lastTime = performance.now();
  // Start loop
  startLoop();
}

// Boot – start the game
reset();
```

Common interpretations:
- "canvas appears blank" → the game is loading but `render()` isn't drawing anything. Check that `startLoop()` actually got called at boot, that `render()` runs every frame, and that you're issuing `ctx.fill*` / `ctx.stroke*` calls per entity each frame (not just clearing the canvas).
- "controls list mismatch" → the `<ul class="game-controls">` in `index.html` and the `controls` array in `meta.json` disagree. Pick one source of truth, edit the other to match exactly.
- "playtest: player.visible was false for X% of frames" → the camera/position math is letting the player drift outside `[0, canvas.width) × [0, canvas.height)`. Either clamp the player or update `player.visible` to reflect the truth (don't just hardcode `true`).
- "playtest: state took invalid value" → `window.__game.state` must be exactly `"menu"`, `"playing"`, or `"gameover"`. No other strings.
- "Required file missing: meta.json" → you didn't write the file. Use your write tool now.
- "pageerror" / "console.error" → an exception is being thrown at runtime. Read the message, locate the line, fix the bug.

- "playtest: state took invalid value" → `window.__game.state` must be exactly `"menu"`, `"playing"`, or `"gameover"`. No other strings.
- "Required file missing: meta.json" → you didn't write the file. Use your write tool now.
- "pageerror" / "console.error" → an exception is being thrown at runtime. Read the message, locate the line, fix the bug.

**Use your write/edit tool to apply the fixes.** Do NOT respond with code in chat — the files on disk are what gets validated.

prompt (12,514 chars)
## Reference example: "snake"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Snake",
  "description": "Eat the food. Don't bite yourself or hit the walls. The snake speeds up as you grow.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or WASD to turn",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "snake", "grid"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Snake</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; image-rendering: pixelated; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Snake</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Eat the food. Don't bite yourself or hit the walls. The snake speeds up as you grow.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or WASD to turn</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const CELL = 25;
  const COLS = 32;            // 32 * 25 = 800
  const ROWS = 24;            // 24 * 25 = 600
  const TICK_START_MS = 140;  // snake step interval at game start
  const TICK_MIN_MS = 60;     // floor speed
  const TICK_SPEEDUP = 3;     // ms shaved off per food eaten

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let snake;          // array of { x, y } in grid coords; snake[0] is head
  let dir;            // { x, y } unit vector
  let nextDir;        // queued direction (so quick double-taps don't U-turn)
  let food;           // { x, y } in grid coords
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let stepIntervalMs;
  let stepAccumulatorMs;
  let phase;          // "playing" | "gameover"
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    snake = [{ x: 10, y: 12 }, { x: 9, y: 12 }, { x: 8, y: 12 }];
    dir = { x: 1, y: 0 };
    nextDir = dir;
    food = randomFreeCell();
    score = 0;
    stepIntervalMs = TICK_START_MS;
    stepAccumulatorMs = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    stepAccumulatorMs += dt * 1000;
    while (stepAccumulatorMs >= stepIntervalMs) {
      stepAccumulatorMs -= stepIntervalMs;
      step();
      if (phase !== "playing") return;
    }
  }

  function step() {
    // Commit queued direction; reject reversals.
    if (nextDir.x !== -dir.x || nextDir.y !== -dir.y) dir = nextDir;

    const head = snake[0];
    const nx = head.x + dir.x;
    const ny = head.y + dir.y;

    // Wall collision -> game over.
    if (nx < 0 || nx >= COLS || ny < 0 || ny >= ROWS) {
      gameOver();
      return;
    }
    // Self collision (skip last segment because it's about to move).
    for (let i = 0; i < snake.length - 1; i++) {
      if (snake[i].x === nx && snake[i].y === ny) {
        gameOver();
        return;
      }
    }

    snake.unshift({ x: nx, y: ny });

    if (nx === food.x && ny === food.y) {
      score++;
      stepIntervalMs = Math.max(TICK_MIN_MS, stepIntervalMs - TICK_SPEEDUP);
      food = randomFreeCell();
      beep(660, 0.06);
    } else {
      snake.pop();
    }
  }

  function gameOver() {
    phase = "gameover";
    if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
    beep(120, 0.18);
  }

  function randomFreeCell() {
    while (true) {
      const c = { x: Math.floor(Math.random() * COLS), y: Math.floor(Math.random() * ROWS) };
      if (!snake.some((s) => s.x === c.x && s.y === c.y)) return c;
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    // Background grid.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "rgba(125,249,255,0.04)";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1;
    for (let x = 0; x <= COLS; x++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(x * CELL, 0); ctx.lineTo(x * CELL, ROWS * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    for (let y = 0; y <= ROWS; y++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(0, y * CELL); ctx.lineTo(COLS * CELL, y * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }

    // Food (pulsing).
    const pulse = 0.85 + 0.15 * Math.sin(performance.now() / 180);
    drawCell(food.x, food.y, `rgba(255,82,174,${pulse.toFixed(3)})`);

    // Snake body.
    for (let i = snake.length - 1; i >= 1; i--) {
      drawCell(snake[i].x, snake[i].y, "#52ffae");
    }
    // Snake head — brighter so the player tracks it instantly.
    drawCell(snake[0].x, snake[0].y, "#9dffd1");

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}` + (score === highScore && score > 0 ? " — new best!" : ""), canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 16);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 44);
    }
  }

  function drawCell(cx, cy, fill) {
    ctx.fillStyle = fill;
    ctx.fillRect(cx * CELL + 1, cy * CELL + 1, CELL - 2, CELL - 2);
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    switch (ev.key) {
      case "ArrowUp": case "w": case "W": nextDir = { x: 0, y: -1 }; break;
      case "ArrowDown": case "s": case "S": nextDir = { x: 0, y: 1 }; break;
      case "ArrowLeft": case "a": case "A": nextDir = { x: -1, y: 0 }; break;
      case "ArrowRight": case "d": case "D": nextDir = { x: 1, y: 0 }; break;
    }
  });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    const head = snake[0];
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                     // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: head.x * CELL + CELL / 2,    // canvas-space center
        y: head.y * CELL + CELL / 2,
        visible: head.x >= 0 && head.x < COLS && head.y >= 0 && head.y < ROWS,
      },
      objective: "Eat the pink food. Don't hit the walls or your own tail.",
    };
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Standard pattern from reference:

let canvas = document.getElementById("game");
let ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
let loopId = null;
let lastTime = performance.now();

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
      // publishState would be called later
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

```
let phase = "menu";
    // === LOOP ==============================================================
    // Animation loop handling.
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();

    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error("loop error", err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    function update(dt) {
      // TODO: game logic to be added later
    }

    function render() {
      // Clear canvas only.
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    }
```

```
    // === LOOP ==============================================================

    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();

    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error("loop error", err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    }
```

Let's implement hazardSpawn function:

```js
function spawnHazard() {
  // spawn at random x at top
  let x, y = 0;
  do {
    x = Math.random() * canvas.width;
  } while (Math.hypot(x - player.x, y - player.y) < SAFE_RADIUS);
  hazards.push({ x, y, vy: HAZARD_SPEED });
}
```

Then in update(dt) we handle spawnAccumulator.

```js
for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
  const h = hazards[i];
  h.y += h.vy * dt;
  if (h.y > canvas.height) hazards.splice(i, 1); // remove offscreen
}
```

We'll replace with:

```
function update(dt) {
  // Game logic placeholder – hazard spawning and movement.
  if (phase !== "playing") return;

  // Spawn hazards based on timer.
  spawnAccumulator -= dt;
  if (spawnAccumulator <= 0) {
    spawnAccumulator = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
    spawnHazard();
  }

  // Update hazard positions.
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.y += h.vy * dt;
    if (h.y > canvas.height) {
      hazards.splice(i, 1); // Remove off-screen hazard.
    }
  }
}
```

But we need to define spawnHazard function somewhere, perhaps after update or before. Let's define after update:

```
function spawnHazard() {
  // Spawn at random x at top, avoiding player start radius.
  let x;
  do {
    x = Math.random() * canvas.width;
  } while (Math.hypot(x - player.x, 0 - player.y) < SAFE_RADIUS);
  hazards.push({ x, y: 0, vy: HAZARD_SPEED });
}
```

if (phase !== "playing") return; // else

for (let i = 0; i < hazards.length; i++) {
  const h = hazards[i];
  // simple collision: distance squared
  const dx = h.x - player.x;
  const dy = h.y - player.y;
  const distSq = dx*dx + dy*dy;
  const radSum = (h.r ?? 10) + (player.r ?? 10);
  if (distSq <= radSum*radSum) {
    // collision
    phase = "gameover";
    // stop loop
    if (loopId !== null) {
      cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      loopId = null;
    }
    break;
  }
}

Now implement update(dt):

function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== "playing") return;
  // move hazards
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.x += h.vx * dt;
    h.y += h.vy * dt;
    // optional: remove hazards that exit canvas? not needed now.
  }
  // spawn hazards based on timer
  hazardSpawnAccumulator += dt;
  while (hazardSpawnAccumulator >= HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL) {
    hazardSpawnAccumulator -= HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL;
    spawnHazard();
  }
}

This ensures hazards move and spawn.

Now spawnHazard implementation:

function spawnHazard() {
  // pick random position within canvas
  let x, y;
  const maxAttempts = 100;
  let attempts = 0;
  do {
    x = Math.random() * canvas.width;
    y = Math.random() * canvas.height;
    attempts++;
    // safety to avoid infinite loop
  } while (Math.hypot(x - player.x, y - player.y) < SAFE_RADIUS && attempts < maxAttempts);
  const dx = player.x - x;
  const dy = player.y - y;
  const len = Math.hypot(dx, dy) || 1;
  const vx = (dx / len) * HAZARD_SPEED;
  const vy = (dy / len) * HAZARD_SPEED;
  hazards.push({ x, y, vx, vy, r: HAZARD_SIZE });
}

We need to ensure we have access to canvas variable - defined earlier.

Now adjust render function later to draw hazards; not required yet.

```
function update(dt) {
    // Placeholder – game logic added later.
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    // Collision detection between player and hazards.
    for (let i = 0; i < hazards.length; i++) {
        const h = hazards[i];
        const dx = h.x - player.x;
        const dy = h.y - player.y;
        const distSq = dx * dx + dy * dy;
        const radSum = h.r + player.r;
        if (distSq < radSum * radSum) {
            // Collision occurred.
            phase = "gameover";
            // Stop the animation loop.
            if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
            break;
        }
    }
}
```

```
function render() {
  // Clear canvas.
  ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  // Background.
  ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  // Draw hazards.
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ff5252"; // red hazards.
  hazards.forEach(h => {
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(h.x, h.y, h.r ?? HAZARD_SIZE, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();
  });
  // Draw player.
  ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff"; // bright cyan.
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.r, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
  // HUD.
  ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
  ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif";
  ctx.textAlign = "left";
  ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
}
```

Add:

```js
// === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!window.audioCtx) window.audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = window.audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = window.audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = "square";
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, window.audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, window.audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(window.audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(window.audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {
    // audio not supported or error, ignore
  }
}
```

---
prompt (5,113 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Echo Trail
Concept: Tap to spawn an echo that follows the exact path you just traveled for a few seconds, destroying any obstacles it contacts while you keep moving forward.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.
